975"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEDearFoot2001745,_975-92"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"sfn","href":"./Template:Sfn"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Dear"},"2":{"wt":"Foot"},"3":{"wt":"2001"},"pp":{"wt":"745, 975"}},"i":0}}]}'><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDearFoot2001745,_975-92" id="mwAzM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAzQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAzU">[</span>89<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAzY">]</span></span></a></sup> In September 1940, Bulgaria demanded <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Dobruja" title="Southern Dobruja" id="mwAzc">Southern Dobruja</a> from Romania with German and Italian support, leading to the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Craiova" title="Treaty of Craiova" id="mwAzg">Treaty of Craiova</a>.<sup about="#mwt367" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Haynes-2000_93-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Haynes-2000"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Haynes-2000-93"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Haynes-2000-93" id="mwAzk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAzo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAzs">[</span>90<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAzw">]</span></span></a></sup> The loss of one-third of Romania's 1939 territory caused a coup against King <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carol_II" title="Carol II" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAz0">Carol II</a>, turning Romania into a fascist dictatorship under Marshal <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ion_Antonescu" title="Ion Antonescu" id="mwAz4">Ion Antonescu</a>, with a course set towards the Axis in the hopes of a German guarantee.<sup about="#mwt368" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-94" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-94"}}'><a href="#cite_note-94" id="mwAz8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA0A"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA0E">[</span>91<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA0I">]</span></span></a></sup> Meanwhile, German–Soviet political relations and economic co-operation<sup about="#mwt350" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFerguson2006367,_376,_379,_417_95-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"group":"","name":"FOOTNOTEFerguson2006367, 376, 379, 417"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEFerguson2006367,_376,_379,_417-95"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"sfn","href":"./Template:Sfn"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Ferguson"},"2":{"wt":"2006"},"pp":{"wt":"367, 376, 379, 417"}},"i":0}}]}'><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFerguson2006367,_376,_379,_417-95" id="mwA0M"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA0Q"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA0U">[</span>92<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA0Y">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt353" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESnyder2010118ff_96-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"group":"","name":"FOOTNOTESnyder2010118ff"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTESnyder2010118ff-96"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"sfn","href":"./Template:Sfn"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Snyder"},"2":{"wt":"2010"},"pp":{"wt":"118ff"}},"i":0}}]}'><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESnyder2010118ff-96" id="mwA0c"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA0g"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA0k">[</span>93<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA0o">]</span></span></a></sup> gradually stalled,<sup about="#mwt356" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKoch1983912–914,_917–920_97-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"group":"","name":"FOOTNOTEKoch1983912–914, 917–920"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKoch1983912–914,_917–920-97"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"sfn","href":"./Template:Sfn"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Koch"},"2":{"wt":"1983"},"pp":{"wt":"912–914, 917–920"}},"i":0}}]}'><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKoch1983912–914,_917–920-97" id="mwA0s"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA0w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA00">[</span>94<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA04">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt359" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERoberts200656_98-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"group":"","name":"FOOTNOTERoberts200656"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTERoberts200656-98"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"sfn","href":"./Template:Sfn"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Roberts"},"2":{"wt":"2006"},"p":{"wt":"56"}},"i":0}}]}'><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERoberts200656-98" id="mwA08"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA1A"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA1E">[</span>95<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA1I">]</span></span></a></sup> and both states began preparations for war.<sup about="#mwt362" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERoberts200659_99-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"group":"","name":"FOOTNOTERoberts200659"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTERoberts200659-99"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"sfn","href":"./Template:Sfn"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Roberts"},"2":{"wt":"2006"},"p":{"wt":"59"}},"i":0}}]}'><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERoberts200659-99" id="mwA1M"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA1Q"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA1U">[</span>96<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA1Y">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="15" id="mwA1c" aria-labelledby="Western_Europe_(1940–1941)"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Western_Europe_(1940–1941)"><span id="Western_Europe_.281940.E2.80.931941.29" typeof="mw:FallbackId"></span>Western Europe (1940–1941)</h3></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt369" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwA1g" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Main","href":"./Template:Main"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Western Front (World War II)"}},"i":0}}]}'><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt370" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Hatnote/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'/></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt369" id="mwA1k">Main article: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Front_(World_War_II)" title="Western Front (World War II)">Western Front (World War II)</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwA1o"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:WWIIEuropeMay40.gif" class="mw-file-description" id="mwA1s"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:WWIIEuropeMay40.gif" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f6/WWIIEuropeMay40.gif/500px-WWIIEuropeMay40.gif?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/f/f6/WWIIEuropeMay40.gif?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail_unscaled 2x" data-file-width="890" data-file-height="690" data-file-type="bitmap" height="271" width="350" class="mw-file-element mw-file-upright" style="--mw-file-upright: 1.4" id="mwA1w"/></a><figcaption id="mwA10">German advance into Belgium and Northern France, 10 May<span typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Entity" about="#mwt371" id="mwA14" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"snd","href":"./Template:Snd"},"params":{},"i":0}}]}'> </span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt371">–</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt371"> </span>4 June 1940, sweeping past the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maginot_Line" title="Maginot Line" id="mwA18">Maginot Line</a> (shown in dark red)</figcaption></figure>

<p id="mwA2A">In April 1940, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Weserübung" title="Operation Weserübung" id="mwA2E">Germany invaded Denmark and Norway</a> to protect shipments of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swedish_iron-ore_industry_during_World_War_II" title="Swedish iron-ore industry during World War II" id="mwA2I">iron ore from Sweden</a>, which the Allies were <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Wilfred" title="Operation Wilfred" id="mwA2M">attempting to cut off</a>.<sup about="#mwt380" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-100" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-100"}}'><a href="#cite_note-100" id="mwA2Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA2U"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA2Y">[</span>97<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA2c">]</span></span></a></sup> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_invasion_of_Denmark_(1940)" title="German invasion of Denmark (1940)" id="mwA2g">Denmark capitulated after six hours</a>, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwegian_campaign" title="Norwegian campaign" id="mwA2k">despite Allied support</a>, Norway was conquered within two months.<sup about="#mwt372" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECommager20049_101-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"group":"","name":"FOOTNOTECommager20049"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTECommager20049-101"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"sfn","href":"./Template:Sfn"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Commager"},"2":{"wt":"2004"},"p":{"wt":"9"}},"i":0}}]}'><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECommager20049-101" id="mwA2o"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA2s"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA2w">[</span>98<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA20">]</span></span></a></sup> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norway_Debate" title="Norway Debate" id="mwA24">British discontent over the Norwegian campaign</a> led to the resignation of Prime Minister <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neville_Chamberlain" title="Neville Chamberlain" id="mwA28">Neville Chamberlain</a>, who was replaced by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winston_Churchill" title="Winston Churchill" id="mwA3A">Winston Churchill</a> on 10<span class="nowrap" about="#mwt375" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwA3E" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"spaces","href":"./Template:Spaces"},"params":{},"i":0}}]}'><span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span></span>May 1940.<sup about="#mwt376" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEReynolds200676_102-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"group":"","name":"FOOTNOTEReynolds200676"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEReynolds200676-102"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"sfn","href":"./Template:Sfn"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Reynolds"},"2":{"wt":"2006"},"p":{"wt":"76"}},"i":0}}]}'><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEReynolds200676-102" id="mwA3I"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA3M"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA3Q">[</span>99<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA3U">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwA3Y">On the same day, Germany <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_France" title="Battle of France" id="mwA3c">launched an offensive against France</a>. To circumvent the strong <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maginot_Line" title="Maginot Line" id="mwA3g">Maginot Line</a> fortifications on the Franco-German border, Germany directed its attack at the neutral nations of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_invasion_of_Belgium_(1940)" title="German invasion of Belgium (1940)" id="mwA3k">Belgium</a>, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_invasion_of_the_Netherlands" title="German invasion of the Netherlands" id="mwA3o">Netherlands</a>, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_invasion_of_Luxembourg" title="German invasion of Luxembourg" id="mwA3s">Luxembourg</a>.<sup about="#mwt381" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEvans2008122–123_103-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"group":"","name":"FOOTNOTEEvans2008122–123"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEEvans2008122–123-103"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"sfn","href":"./Template:Sfn"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Evans"},"2":{"wt":"2008"},"pp":{"wt":"122–123"}},"i":0}}]}'><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEvans2008122–123-103" id="mwA3w"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA30"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA34">[</span>100<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA38">]</span></span></a></sup> The Germans carried out a flanking manoeuvre through the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ardennes" title="Ardennes" id="mwA4A">Ardennes</a> region,<sup about="#mwt384" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKeegan199759–60_104-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"group":"","name":"FOOTNOTEKeegan199759–60"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeegan199759–60-104"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"sfn","href":"./Template:Sfn"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Keegan"},"2":{"wt":"1997"},"pp":{"wt":"59–60"}},"i":0}}]}'><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeegan199759–60-104" id="mwA4E"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA4I"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA4M">[</span>101<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA4Q">]</span></span></a></sup> which was mistakenly perceived by the Allies as an impenetrable natural barrier against armoured vehicles.<sup about="#mwt387" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERegan2004152_105-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"group":"","name":"FOOTNOTERegan2004152"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTERegan2004152-105"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"sfn","href":"./Template:Sfn"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Regan"},"2":{"wt":"2004"},"p":{"wt":"152"}},"i":0}}]}'><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERegan2004152-105" id="mwA4U"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA4Y"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA4c">[</span>102<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA4g">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt390" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELiddell_Hart197748_106-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"group":"","name":"FOOTNOTELiddell Hart197748"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTELiddell_Hart197748-106"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"sfn","href":"./Template:Sfn"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Liddell Hart"},"2":{"wt":"1977"},"p":{"wt":"48"}},"i":0}}]}'><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELiddell_Hart197748-106" id="mwA4k"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA4o"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA4s">[</span>103<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA4w">]</span></span></a></sup> By successfully implementing new <i id="mwA40"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blitzkrieg" title="Blitzkrieg" id="mwA44">Blitzkrieg</a></i> tactics, the <i id="mwA48">Wehrmacht</i> rapidly advanced to the Channel and cut off the Allied forces in Belgium, trapping the bulk of the Allied armies in a cauldron on the Franco-Belgian border near Lille. The United Kingdom was able <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dunkirk_evacuation" title="Dunkirk evacuation" id="mwA5A">to evacuate a significant number of Allied troops</a> from the continent by early June, although they had to abandon almost all their equipment.<sup about="#mwt393" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKeegan199766–67_107-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"group":"","name":"FOOTNOTEKeegan199766–67"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeegan199766–67-107"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"sfn","href":"./Template:Sfn"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Keegan"},"2":{"wt":"1997"},"pp":{"wt":"66–67"}},"i":0}}]}'><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeegan199766–67-107" id="mwA5E"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA5I"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA5M">[</span>104<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA5Q">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwA5U">On 10 June, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_invasion_of_France" title="Italian invasion of France" id="mwA5Y">Italy invaded France</a>, declaring war on both France and the United Kingdom.<sup about="#mwt396" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEOveryWheatcroft1999207_108-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"group":"","name":"FOOTNOTEOveryWheatcroft1999207"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEOveryWheatcroft1999207-108"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"sfn","href":"./Template:Sfn"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Overy"},"2":{"wt":"Wheatcroft"},"3":{"wt":"1999"},"p":{"wt":"207"}},"i":0}}]}'><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOveryWheatcroft1999207-108" id="mwA5c"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA5g"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA5k">[</span>105<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA5o">]</span></span></a></sup> The Germans turned south against the weakened French army, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_in_World_War_II" title="Paris in World War II" id="mwA5s">Paris</a> fell to them on 14<span class="nowrap" about="#mwt399" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwA5w" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"spaces","href":"./Template:Spaces"},"params":{},"i":0}}]}'><span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span></span>June. Eight days later <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armistice_of_22_June_1940" title="Armistice of 22 June 1940" id="mwA50">France signed an armistice with Germany</a>; it was divided into <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_military_administration_in_occupied_France_during_World_War_II" title="German military administration in occupied France during World War II" id="mwA54">German</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_occupation_of_France" title="Italian occupation of France" id="mwA58">Italian occupation zones</a>,<sup about="#mwt400" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUmbreit1991311_109-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"group":"","name":"FOOTNOTEUmbreit1991311"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEUmbreit1991311-109"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"sfn","href":"./Template:Sfn"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Umbreit"},"2":{"wt":"1991"},"p":{"wt":"311"}},"i":0}}]}'><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUmbreit1991311-109" id="mwA6A"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA6E"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA6I">[</span>106<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA6M">]</span></span></a></sup> and an unoccupied <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rump_state" title="Rump state" id="mwA6Q">rump state</a> under the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vichy_Regime" title="Vichy Regime" class="mw-redirect" id="mwA6U">Vichy Regime</a>, which, though officially neutral, was generally aligned with Germany. France kept its fleet, which the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attack_on_Mers-el-Kébir" title="Attack on Mers-el-Kébir" id="mwA6Y">United Kingdom attacked</a> on 3<span class="nowrap" about="#mwt403" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwA6c" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"spaces","href":"./Template:Spaces"},"params":{},"i":0}}]}'><span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span></span>July in an attempt to prevent its seizure by Germany.<sup about="#mwt404" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrown2004198_110-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"group":"","name":"FOOTNOTEBrown2004198"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrown2004198-110"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"sfn","href":"./Template:Sfn"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Brown"},"2":{"wt":"2004"},"p":{"wt":"198"}},"i":0}}]}'><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrown2004198-110" id="mwA6g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA6k"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA6o">[</span>107<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA6s">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwA6w"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:St_Paul_destroyed.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwA60"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:St_Paul_destroyed.jpg" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/St_Paul_destroyed.jpg/250px-St_Paul_destroyed.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="laof the art in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_language_generation" title="Natural language generation" id="mwAT4">natural language generation</a>. At the end of 2022, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ChatGPT" title="ChatGPT" id="mwAT8">ChatGPT</a>, a chatbot based on a fine-tuned variant of GPT-3.5, became unexpectedly<sup about="#mwt160" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-43" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-43"}}'><a href="#cite_note-43" id="mwAUA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAUE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUI">[</span>41<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUM">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt163" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-44" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-44"}}'><a href="#cite_note-44" id="mwAUQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAUU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUY">[</span>42<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUc">]</span></span></a></sup> popular, triggering a boom around <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Large_language_model" title="Large language model" id="mwAUg">large language models</a>.<sup about="#mwt166" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-gpt12_45-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"gpt12"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-gpt12-45"}}'><a href="#cite_note-gpt12-45" id="mwAUk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAUo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUs">[</span>43<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUw">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt169" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-ngEG3_46-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"ngEG3"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-ngEG3-46"}}'><a href="#cite_note-ngEG3-46" id="mwAU0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAU4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAU8">[</span>44<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAVE">Transformers have been applied in modalities beyond text. Four days after the publication of "Attention is All You Need", a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multimodal_learning" title="Multimodal learning" id="mwAVI">multimodal</a> transformer architecture, MultiModel, was published by most authors of that paper.<sup about="#mwt172" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-47" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-47"}}'><a href="#cite_note-47" id="mwAVM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAVQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVU">[</span>45<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVY">]</span></span></a></sup> Other examples include the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vision_transformer" title="Vision transformer" id="mwAVc">vision transformer</a>,<sup about="#mwt175" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-auto2_48-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"auto2"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-auto2-48"}}'><a href="#cite_note-auto2-48" id="mwAVg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAVk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVo">[</span>46<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVs">]</span></span></a></sup> speech recognition,<sup about="#mwt176" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Gulati2020_49-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Gulati2020"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Gulati2020-49" id="mwAVw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAV0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAV4">[</span>47<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAV8">]</span></span></a></sup> robotics,<sup about="#mwt179" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:10_7-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":10"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:10-7"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:10-7" id="mwAWA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAWE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWI">[</span>7<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWM">]</span></span></a></sup> and multimodal.<sup about="#mwt182" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-choromanski2020_50-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"choromanski2020"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-choromanski2020-50"}}'><a href="#cite_note-choromanski2020-50" id="mwAWQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAWU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWY">[</span>48<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWc">]</span></span></a></sup> The vision transformer, in turn, stimulated new developments in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convolutional_neural_network" title="Convolutional neural network" id="mwAWg">convolutional neural networks</a>.<sup about="#mwt185" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-51" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-51"}}'><a href="#cite_note-51" id="mwAWk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAWo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWs">[</span>49<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWw">]</span></span></a></sup> Image and video generators like <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DALL-E" title="DALL-E" id="mwAW0">DALL-E</a> (2021), <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stable_Diffusion" title="Stable Diffusion" id="mwAW4">Stable Diffusion 3</a> (2024),<sup about="#mwt188" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:62_52-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":62"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:62-52"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:62-52" id="mwAW8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAXA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAXE">[</span>50<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAXI">]</span></span></a></sup> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sora_(text-to-video_model)" title="Sora (text-to-video model)" id="mwAXM">Sora</a> (2024), use transformers to analyse input data (like text prompts) by breaking it down into "tokens" and then calculating the relevance between each token using self-attention, which helps the model understand the context and relationships within the data.</p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="6" id="mwAXQ" aria-labelledby="Training"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Training">Training</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transformer_(deep_learning)&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Training"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<section data-mw-section-id="7" id="mwAXU" aria-labelledby="Methods_for_stabilizing_training"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Methods_for_stabilizing_training">Methods for stabilizing training</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transformer_(deep_learning)&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Methods for stabilizing training"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAXY">The plain transformer architecture had difficulty in converging. In the original paper,<sup about="#mwt189" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-2017_Attention_Is_All_You_Need_1-4" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"2017_Attention_Is_All_You_Need"}}'><a href="#cite_note-2017_Attention_Is_All_You_Need-1" id="mwAXc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAXg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAXk">[</span>1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAXo">]</span></span></a></sup> the authors recommended using <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Learning_rate" title="Learning rate" id="mwAXs">learning rate</a> warmup. That is, the learning rate should linearly scale up from 0 to maximal value for the first part of the training (usually recommended to be 2% of the total number of training steps), before decaying again.</p>

<p id="mwAXw">A 2020 paper found that using <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Layer_normalization" title="Layer normalization" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAX0">layer normalization</a> <i id="mwAX4">before</i> (instead of after) multihead attention and feedforward layers stabilizes training, not requiring learning rate warmup.<sup about="#mwt192" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-auto1_53-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"auto1"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-auto1-53"}}'><a href="#cite_note-auto1-53" id="mwAX8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAYA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYE">[</span>51<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYI">]</span></span></a></sup> This is the "pre-LN Transformer" and is more commonly used, compared to the original "post-LN Transformer".</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="8" id="mwAYM" aria-labelledby="Pretrain-finetune"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Pretrain-finetune">Pretrain-finetune</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transformer_(deep_learning)&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Pretrain-finetune"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAYQ">Transformers typically are first pretrained by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-supervised_learning" title="Self-supervised learning" id="mwAYU">self-supervised learning</a> on a large generic dataset, followed by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supervised_learning" title="Supervised learning" id="mwAYY">supervised</a> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fine-tuning_(deep_learning)" title="Fine-tuning (deep learning)" id="mwAYc">fine-tuning</a> on a small task-specific dataset. The pretrain dataset is typically an unlabeled large corpus, such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pile_(dataset)" title="The Pile (dataset)" id="mwAYg">The Pile</a>. Tasks for pretraining and fine-tuning commonly include:</p>
<ul id="mwAYk"><li id="mwAYo"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Language_modeling" title="Language modeling" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAYs">language modeling</a><sup about="#mwt193" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:6_13-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":6"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:6-13" id="mwAYw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAY0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAY4">[</span>13<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAY8">]</span></span></a></sup></li>
<li id="mwAZA">next-sentence prediction<sup about="#mwt194" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:6_13-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":6"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:6-13" id="mwAZE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAZI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZM">[</span>13<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZQ">]</span></span></a></sup></li>
<li id="mwAZU"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Question_answering" title="Question answering" id="mwAZY">question answering</a><sup about="#mwt195" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:7_3-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":7"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:7-3" id="mwAZc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAZg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZk">[</span>3<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZo">]</span></span></a></sup></li>
<li id="mwAZs"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural-language_understanding" title="Natural-language understanding" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAZw">reading comprehension</a></li>
<li id="mwAZ0"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sentiment_analysis" title="Sentiment analysis" id="mwAZ4">sentiment analysis</a><sup about="#mwt196" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-2017_Attention_Is_All_You_Need_1-5" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"2017_Attention_Is_All_You_Need"}}'><a href="#cite_note-2017_Attention_Is_All_You_Need-1" id="mwAZ8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAaA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAaE">[</span>1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAaI">]</span></span></a></sup></li>
<li id="mwAaM"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Text_Summaries" title="Text Summaries" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAaQ">paraphrasing</a><sup about="#mwt197" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-2017_Attention_Is_All_You_Need_1-6" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"2017_Attention_Is_All_You_Need"}}'><a href="#cite_note-2017_Attention_Is_All_You_Need-1" id="mwAaU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAaY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAac">[</span>1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAag">]</span></span></a></sup></li></ul>
<p id="mwAak">The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T5_(language_model)" title="T5 (language model)" id="mwAao">T5 transformer</a> report<sup about="#mwt200" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Raffel_Shazeer_2020_Exploring_the_Limits_54-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Raffel Shazeer 2020 Exploring the Limits"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Raffel_Shazeer_2020_Exploring_the_Limits-54"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Raffel_Shazeer_2020_Exploring_the_Limits-54" id="mwAas"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAaw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAa0">[</span>52<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAa4">]</span></span></a></sup> documents a large number of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_language" title="Natural language" id="mwAa8">natural language</a> pretraining tasks. Some examples are:</p>

<ul id="mwAbA"><li id="mwAbE">restoring or repairing incomplete or corrupted text. For example, the input, <i id="mwAbI">"Thank you<span typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Entity" about="#mwt201" id="mwAbM" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"nnbsp","href":"./Template:Nnbsp"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"~~"}},"i":0}}]}'> </span><span about="#mwt201">~~</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt201"> </span>me to your party<span typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Entity" about="#mwt202" id="mwAbQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"nnbsp","href":"./Template:Nnbsp"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"~~"}},"i":0}}]}'> </span><span about="#mwt202">~~</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt202"> </span>week",</i> might generate the output, <i id="mwAbU">"Thank you <b id="mwAbY">for inviting</b> me to your party <b id="mwAbc">last</b> week".</i></li>
<li id="mwAbg">translation between natural languages (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Machine_translation" title="Machine translation" id="mwAbk">machine translation</a>)</li>
<li id="mwAbo">judging the pragmatic acceptability of natural language. For example, the following sentence might be judged "not acceptable",<sup about="#mwt205" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-55" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-55"}}'><a href="#cite_note-55" id="mwAbs"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAbw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAb0">[</span>53<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAb4">]</span></span></a></sup> because even though it is syntactically well-formed, it is improbable in ordinary human usage: <i id="mwAb8">The course is jumping well.</i></li></ul>

<p id="mwAcA">While each of these tasks is trivial or obvious for human native speakers of the language (or languages), they have typically proved challenging for previous generations of machine learning architecture.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="9" id="mwAcE" aria-labelledby="Tasks"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Tasks">Tasks</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transformer_(deep_learning)&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Tasks"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt206" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAcI" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"See also","href":"./Template:See_also"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Large language model#Evaluation"}},"i":0}}]}'><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt207" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Hatnote/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'/></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt206" id="mwAcM">See also: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Large_language_model#Evaluation" title="Large language model">Large language model §<span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>Evaluation</a></div>
<p id="mwAcQ">In general, there are three classes of language modelling tasks: "masked",<sup about="#mwt210" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:5_56-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":5"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:5-56"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:5-56" id="mwAcU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAcY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAcc">[</span>54<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAcg">]</span></span></a></sup> "autoregressive",<sup about="#mwt213" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:8_57-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":8"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:8-57"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:8-57" id="mwAck"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAco"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAcs">[</span>55<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAcw">]</span></span></a></sup> and "prefixLM".<sup about="#mwt214" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:4_58-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":4"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:4-58" id="mwAc0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAc4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAc8">[</span>56<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAdA">]</span></span></a></sup> These classes are independent of a specific modeling architecture such as transformer, but they are often discussed in the context of transformer.</p>

<p id="mwAdE">In a masked task,<sup about="#mwt215" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:5_56-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":5"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:5-56" id="mwAdI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAdM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAdQ">[</span>54<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAdU">]</span></span></a></sup> one or more of the tokens is masked out, and the model would produce a probability distribution predicting what the masked-out tokens are based on the context. The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loss_function" title="Loss function" id="mwAdY">loss function</a> for the task is typically sum of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perplexity" title="Perplexity" id="mwAdc">log-perplexities</a> for the masked-out tokens: <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-block" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt216" id="mwAdg" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{"display":"block"},"body":{"extsrc":"\\text{Loss} = -\\sum_{t\\in\\text{masked tokens}}\\ln(\\text{probability of }t\\text{ conditional on its context}) "}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-display mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" display="block" alttext="{\displaystyle {\text{Loss}}=-\sum _{t\in {\text{masked tokens}}}\ln({\text{probability of }}t{\text{ conditional on its context}})}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
          <mtext>Loss</mtext>
        </mrow>
        <mo>=</mo>
        <mo>−<!-- − --></mo>

          </mrow>
        </munder>
        <mi>ln</mi>
        <mo>⁡<!-- ⁡ --></mo>
        <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
        <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
          <mtext>probability of </mtext>
        </mrow>
        <mi>t</mi>

          <mtext> conditional on its context</mtext>

    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle {\text{Loss}}=-\sum _{t\in {\text{masked tokens}}}\ln({\text{probability of }}t{\text{ conditional on its context}})}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/55f0855cde2d171c96b77251ce43de9ee3cfd4e8" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-display mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -3.171ex; width:67.226ex; height:5.676ex;" alt="{\displaystyle {\text{Loss}}=-\sum _{t\in {\text{masked tokens}}}\ln({\text{probability of }}t{\text{ conditional on its context}})}"/></span>and the model is trained to minimize this loss function. The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BERT_(language_model)" title="BERT (language model)" id="mwAdk">BERT series of models</a> are trained for masked token prediction and another task. ("Masked" as in "masked language modelling" is not "masked" as in "<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="#Masked_attention" class="mw-selflink-fragment" id="mwAdo">masked attention</a>".)</p>

<p id="mwAds">In an autoregressive task,<sup about="#mwt217" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:8_57-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":8"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:8-57" id="mwAdw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAd0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAd4">[</span>55<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAd8">]</span></span></a></sup> the entire sequence is masked at first, and the model produces a probability distribution for the first token. Then the first token is revealed and the model predicts the second token, and so on. The loss function for the task is still typically the same. The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Generative_pre-trained_transformer" title="Generative pre-trained transformer" id="mwAeA">GPT series of models</a> are trained by autoregressive tasks.</p>

<p id="mwAeE">In a prefixLM task,<sup about="#mwt218" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:4_58-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":4"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:4-58" id="mwAeI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAeM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAeQ">[</span>56<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAeU">]</span></span></a></sup> the sequence is divided into two parts. The first part is presented as context, and the model predicts the first token of the second part. Then that would be revealed, and the model predicts the second token, and so on. The loss function for the task is still typically the same. The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T5_(language_model)" title="T5 (language model)" id="mwAeY">T5 series of models</a> are trained by prefixLM tasks. ("PrefixLM" as in "prefix language modeling" is not "prefixLM" as in "<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="#prefixLM" class="mw-selflink-fragment" id="mwAec">prefix language model</a>".)</p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="10" id="mwAeg" aria-labelledby="Architecture"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Architecture">Architecture</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transformer_(deep_learning)&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Architecture"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAek">All transformers have the same primary components:</p>
<ul id="mwAeo"><li id="mwAes">Tokenizers, which convert text into tokens.</li>
<li id="mwAew">Embedding layer, which converts tokens and positions of the tokens into vector representations.</li>
<li id="mwAe0">Transformer layers, which carry out repeated transformations on the vector representations, extracting more and more linguistic information. These consist of alternating attention and feedforward layers. There are two major types of transformer layers: encoder layers and decoder layers, with further variants.</li>
<li id="mwAe4">Un-embedding layer, which converts the final vector representations back to a probability distribution over the tokens.</li></ul>
<p id="mwAe8">The following description follows exactly the transformer as described in the original paper. There are variants, described in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="#Subsequent_work" class="mw-selflink-fragment" id="mwAfA">following section</a>.</p>

<p id="mwAfE">By convention, we write all vectors as row vectors. For example, pushing a vector through a linear layer means multiplying it by a weight matrix on the right, as <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt219" id="mwAfI" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"xW"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle xW}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mi>x</mi>
        <mi>W</mi>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle xW}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/b366d97326adb9cbb74e22c5ee0966dd055cd2dc" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.338ex; width:3.765ex; height:2.176ex;" alt="{\displaystyle xW}"/></span>.</p>

<section data-mw-section-id="11" id="mwAfM" aria-labelledby="Tokenization"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Tokenization">Tokenization</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transformer_(deep_learning)&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Tokenization"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAfQ">As the transformer architecture natively consists of operations over numbers (matrix multiplications, dot products, activation functions) rather than over text, there must first be a mapping from any input text to some numerical representation. This happens in three steps.</p>

<p id="mwAfU">First, the input text is treated by a <i id="mwAfY">preprocessor</i>, which performs both textual transformations and splits the text into coarse-grained segments called <i id="mwAfc">pretokens</i>. The latter is referred to as <i id="mwAfg">pretokenization</i>. Second, each pretoken is segmented further into <i id="mwAfk">tokens</i> by a <i id="mwAfo">tokenizer</i> that expects to only see pretokens output by its preprocessor. Each token it produces is a string of one or more characters belonging to a finite set of strings called the <i id="mwAfs">vocabulary</i> <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt220" id="mwAfw" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"V"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle V}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mi>V</mi>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle V}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/af0f6064540e84211d0ffe4dac72098adfa52845" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.338ex; width:1.787ex; height:2.176ex;" alt="{\displaystyle V}"/></span>. Third, because the vocabulary is finite and known beforehand, each token can be assigned an integer identifier, and this mapping is applied to the sequence of tokens to represent any input text as a numerical sequence. Since this mapping is bijective, the output side can produce a sequence of integer identifiers which can then be turned back into tokens. After undoing some of the preprocessing, the result is again legible text.</p>

<p id="mwAf0">Training a tokenizer (sometimes referred to as <i id="mwAf4">vocabularization</i>) means finding a suitable vocabulary <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt221" id="mwAf8" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"V"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle V}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mi>V</mi>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle V}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/af0f6064540e84211d0ffe4dac72098adfa52845" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.338ex; width:1.787ex; height:2.176ex;" alt="{\displaystyle V}"/></span>, but also learning how to use it, since any given string <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt222" id="mwAgA" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"s"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle s}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mi>s</mi>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle s}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/01d131dfd7673938b947072a13a9744fe997e632" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.338ex; width:1.09ex; height:1.676ex;" alt="{\displaystyle s}"/></span> of length <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt223" id="mwAgE" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"|s|"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle |s|}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
          <mo stretchy="false">|</mo>
        </mrow>
        <mi>s</mi>
        <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
          <mo stretchy="false">|</mo>
        </mrow>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle |s|}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/0ae65dea0cc836140252292ee9adf2d8b5102055" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.838ex; width:2.384ex; height:2.843ex;" alt="{\displaystyle |s|}"/></span> has <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt224" id="mwAgI" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"2^{|s|-1}"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle 2^{|s|-1}}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <msup>
          <mn>2</mn>
          <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
            <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
              <mo stretchy="false">|</mo>
            </mrow>
            <mi>s</mi>
            <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
              <mo stretchy="false">|</mo>
            </mrow>
            <mo>−<!-- − --></mo>
            <mn>1</mn>
          </mrow>
        </msup>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle 2^{|s|-1}}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/3d844850486603030caed10d1c3d330d604770b8" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.338ex; width:5.181ex; height:2.843ex;" alt="{\displaystyle 2^{|s|-1}}"/></span> hypothetical segmentations, some of which containing segments that are not in the vocabulary. The most important hyperparameter during vocabularization is the <i id="mwAgM">vocabulary size</i> <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt225" id="mwAgQ" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"|V|"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle |V|}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
          <mo stretchy="false">|</mo>
        </mrow>
        <mi>V</mi>
        <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
          <mo stretchy="false">|</mo>
        </mrow>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle |V|}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/9ddcffc28643ac01a14dd0fb32c3157859e365a7" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.838ex; width:3.081ex; height:2.843ex;" alt="{\displaystyle |V|}"/></span>: when it is small, the learned vocabulary generally consists of characters and smaller strings, and words will be segmented into many tokens. At larger sizes, it becomes affordable to dedicate tokens to full words, although depending on the preprocessor and tokenizer, it is not necessarily the case that large vocabularies will always use the largest token(s) available to segment a word.</p>

<p id="mwAgU">Because tokens are not always full words, they may also be referred to as <i id="mwAgY">subwords</i> and tokenization algorithms may be referred to as <i id="mwAgc">subword tokenizers</i>. This is also to differentiate these systems from <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lexical_analysis" title="Lexical analysis" id="mwAgg">traditional terminology</a> used in older information retrieval and natural language processing systems, where "tokenization" was used to denote what is today called "pretokenization" (very crudely: splitting into words). In tokenizers that produce tokens that are <i id="mwAgk">not</i> part of the vocabulary, a special token that does belong to the vocabulary is used as a generic stand-in, written as "[UNK]" for "unknown". In principle, any string could be hidden by such an [UNK]. Indeed, in information retrieval, pretokenizers were themselves used as tokenizers (and also called "tokenizers") with a word-level vocabulary that contained an [UNK].</p>

<p id="mwAgo">Commonly used subword tokenization algorithms are <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byte_pair_encoding" title="Byte pair encoding" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAgs">byte pair encoding</a> (BPE) and the unigram language model (ULM), which each include a vocabularization algorithm and a dedicated segmentation algorithm. There also exist several segmentation algorithms that require no learning and can be applied given a vocabulary (produced by BPE or ULM, for example), like greedily recognising tokens in a pretoken by moving through it left-to-right. Well-known software implementations of subword tokenizers are <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hugging_Face" title="Hugging Face" id="mwAgw">Hugging Face</a>'s <code id="mwAg0">tokenizers</code> Python package implemented in Rust, and the <code id="mwAg4">sentencepiece</code> Python package implemented in C++. The latter package is named as such because one of its configuration options allows disabling the built-in pretokenizer, hence effectively making entire sentences a pretoken and thus having the tokenizer see entire sentences, rather than individual words.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="12" id="mwAg8" aria-labelledby="Embedding"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Embedding">Embedding</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transformer_(deep_learning)&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Embedding"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt226" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAhA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Further","href":"./Template:Further"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Word embedding"}},"i":0}}]}'><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt227" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Hatnote/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'/></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt226" id="mwAhE">Further information: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Word_embedding" title="Word embedding">Word embedding</a></div>
<p id="mwAhI">Each integer token identifier is converted into an embedding vector via a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lookup_table" title="Lookup table" id="mwAhM">lookup table</a>. Equivalently stated, it multiplies a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One-hot" title="One-hot" id="mwAhQ">one-hot</a> representation of the token identifier by an embedding matrix <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt228" id="mwAhU" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"M"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle M}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mi>M</mi>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle M}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/f82cade9898ced02fdd08712e5f0c0151758a0dd" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.338ex; width:2.442ex; height:2.176ex;" alt="{\displaystyle M}"/></span>. For example, if the input token's identifier is <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt229" id="mwAhY" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"3"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle 3}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mn>3</mn>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle 3}</annotation>
  </semantics>

<p id="mwAhk">The dimension of an embedding vector is called <i id="mwAho">hidden size</i> or <i id="mwAhs">embedding size</i> and written as <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt232" id="mwAhw" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"d_{\\text{emb}}"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle d_{\text{emb}}}">

</section><section data-mw-section-id="13" id="mwAiY" aria-labelledby="Un-embedding"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Un-embedding">Un-embedding</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transformer_(deep_learning)&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Un-embedding"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAic">An un-embedding layer is almost the reverse of an embedding layer. Whereas an embedding layer converts a token identifier into a vector, an un-embedding layer converts a vector into a probability distribution over tokens.</p>
<figure class="mw-halign-center" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwAig"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Top_token_probabilities,_chain_of_thought_response_only,_for_GPT-OSS_(20b).svg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwAik"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Top_token_probabilities,_chain_of_thought_response_only,_for_GPT-OSS_(20b).svg" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/51/Top_token_probabilities%2C_chain_of_thought_response_only%2C_for_GPT-OSS_%2820b%29.svg/960px-Top_token_probabilities%2C_chain_of_thought_response_only%2C_for_GPT-OSS_%2820b%29.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/51/Top_token_probabilities%2C_chain_of_thought_response_only%2C_for_GPT-OSS_%2820b%29.svg/1920px-Top_token_probabilities%2C_chain_of_thought_response_only%2C_for_GPT-OSS_%2820b%29.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" data-file-width="1728" data-file-height="384" data-file-type="drawing" height="178" width="800" class="mw-file-element" id="mwAio"/></a><figcaption id="mwAis">An illustration of the top 16 token probabilities at temperature 1, for each output token in the chain-of-thought response, with colour representing how that output differs from the same prompt but at temperature 0.</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwAiw">The un-embedding layer is a linear-<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Softmax_function" title="Softmax function" id="mwAi0">softmax</a> layer:<span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-block" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt236" id="mwAi4" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{"display":"block"},"body":{"extsrc":"\\mathrm{UnEmbed}(x) = \\mathrm{softmax}(xW + b)"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-display mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" display="block" alttext="{\displaystyle \mathrm {UnEmbed} (x)=\mathrm {softmax} (xW+b)}">

Some architectures use the transpose of the embedding matrix <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt238" id="mwAjA" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"M"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle M}">

      </mstyle>

  </semantics>

This practice is called <i id="mwAjI">weight tying</i>.<sup about="#mwt242" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-59" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-59"}}'><a href="#cite_note-59" id="mwAjM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAjQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAjU">[</span>57<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAjY">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="14" id="mwAjc" aria-labelledby="Positional_encoding"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Positional_encoding">Positional encoding</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transformer_(deep_learning)&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Positional encoding"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwAjg"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Absolute_positional_encoding.png" class="mw-file-description" id="mwAjk"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Absolute_positional_encoding.png" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a9/Absolute_positional_encoding.png/250px-Absolute_positional_encoding.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a9/Absolute_positional_encoding.png/500px-Absolute_positional_encoding.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" data-file-width="3200" data-file-height="1600" data-file-type="bitmap" height="125" width="250" class="mw-file-element" id="mwAjo"/></a><figcaption id="mwAjs">Illustration of (absolute) positional encoding with parameters <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt243" id="mwAjw" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"N=10000, d=100"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle N=10000,d=100}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">

        <mn>10000</mn>
        <mo>,</mo>
        <mi>d</mi>
        <mo>=</mo>
        <mn>100</mn>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle N=10000,d=100}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/fe83017b9728026bf6d2bae4a357041d198ac494" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.671ex; width:19.81ex; height:2.509ex;" alt="{\displaystyle N=10000,d=100}"/></span></figcaption></figure>

<p id="mwAj0">A positional encoding is a fixed-size vector representation of the relative positions of tokens within a sequence: it provides the transformer model with information about <i id="mwAj4">where</i> the words are in the input sequence. This induces a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inductive_bias" title="Inductive bias" id="mwAj8">bias</a> towards the order of the input sequence, so that, for example, the input sequence "<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Man_bites_dog" title="Man bites dog" id="mwAkA">man bites dog</a>" is processed differently from "dog bites man".</p>

<p id="mwAkE">The positional encoding is defined as a function of type <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt244" id="mwAkI" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"f: \\R \\to \\R^d"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle f:\mathbb {R} \to \mathbb {R} ^{d}}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mi>f</mi>
        <mo>:</mo>
        <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
          <mi mathvariant="double-struck">R</mi>
        </mrow>
        <mo stretchy="false">→<!-- → --></mo>
        <msup>
          <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
            <mi mathvariant="double-struck">R</mi>

            <mi>d</mi>
          </mrow>
        </msup>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle f:\mathbb {R} \to \mathbb {R} ^{d}}</annotation>
  </semantics>

<li about="#cite_note-19" id="cite_note-19" data-mw-footnote-number="19"><span class="mw-cite-backlink" id="mwBjc"><a href="#cite_ref-19" rel="mw:referencedBy" id="mwBjg"><span class="mw-linkback-text" id="mwBjk">↑</span></a></span> <span id="mw-reference-text-cite_note-19" class="mw-reference-text reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1333433106" about="#mwt101" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles mw:Transclusion" id="mwBjo" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Citation/CS1/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"cite book ","href":"./Template:Cite_book"},"params":{"first":{"wt":"Carl"},"last":{"wt":"Sagan"},"author-link":{"wt":"Carl Sagan"},"title":{"wt":"The Demon-Haunted World: Science as a Candle in the Dark"},"page":{"wt":"249"},"publisher":{"wt":"Ballantine Books"},"year":{"wt":"1996"},"isbn":{"wt":"0-345-40946-9"},"title-link":{"wt":"The Demon-Haunted World"},"quote":{"wt":"\"For most physics students, (the \"mathematical underpinning\" of quantum mechanics) might occupy them from, say, third grade to early graduate school{{snd}}roughly 15 years. ...
